ENSURE REGISTRATION OF EVERY BIRTH AND DEATH
                                                 PREFACE

         Birth and Deaths are the two most important vital events that define life of an individual. Not
only do they describe the legal existence of an individual, registration of these events is a primary
source of vital data of the population to which they belong. Civil Registration is a continuous,
permanent, compulsory recording of the occurrence and characteristics of vital events, like births,
deaths and still births. These vital statistics are invaluable for planning, monitoring and evaluating
various programmes related to primary health care, family planning, maternal and child health,
education etc. Information collected through the registration process about vital events provides
useful and important statistics.

2. This issue of the Report on `Vital Statistics of India based on the Civil Registration System' for
the year 2022 provides an overview of the working of the Civil Registration System (CRS) in the
country and presents a compilation of data on registered births, deaths, infant deaths and still births
with rural-urban & sex-wise break-up and sex ratio at birth based on Civil Registration Records.
Based on the Annual Reports of the States/UTs, the report for the year 2022 containing key
information of events pertaining to the time period 1st January 2022 to 31st December 2022 has been
brought out for the convenience of users.

3. The publication is divided into two parts. Part I provides a brief review of the Civil Registration
System and contains a summary analysis of data on registered births, deaths, infant deaths and sex
ratio at birth based on registered births at the State level. Part II consists of the main tables which
provide State and District-wise data on registered births, deaths, infant deaths and still births with
rural-urban & sex-wise break-up.

4. The data source for the report is the statutory Annual Statistical Report prepared by the Chief
Registrars of Births & Deaths of States/UTs. I take this opportunity to express my sincere gratitude
to all the Chief Registrars of Births & Deaths of various States/UTs for their kind cooperation in
providing the requisite information. I am thankful to each one of them for their invaluable and
continuing cooperation. I am hopeful that more dynamic implementation of the provisions of the RBD
Act-1969 shall help reduce the time gap and establish this as a reliable source of information on Birth
and Death registration in the country.

5. The report has been prepared by the CRS Section of this office. I would like to place on record
my appreciation for the efforts put in by all the officers and staff working in the CRS Section of the
Office of the Registrar General and the Officers/Staff connected with the Civil Registration System in
the States/UTs.

New Delhi        Mritunjay Kumar Narayan
June, 2025          Registrar General, India

Registrar General, India (ORGI) has only compiled and collated the data and presented it in

of this Office.

ix
                       Overview of Civil Registration System (CRS)

1. Registration of birth is a right of every child and is the first step towards establishing
         her/his legal identity.

2. It is compulsory to report births and deaths to the Registrar of Births and Deaths under the
         Registration of Births and Deaths Act, 1969 (Act No. 18 of 1969).

3. Births and deaths are registered only at the place of their occurrence.

4. Head of the household is responsible to report births and deaths that occurred in the house
         to the Registrar of Births and Deaths of the concerned area.

5. It is the responsibility of the officer in charge or any person authorised by her/him to report
         the births and deaths that occur in hospitals, nursing homes, health centres etc. to the
         Registrar of Births and Deaths of the concerned area.

6. Births and deaths are reported in the prescribed forms - Form 1 for live birth; Form 2 for
         death and Form 3 for still birth.

7. Midwife or any other medical or health attendant who has attended or was present at the
         time of birth or death are duty bound to notify the concerned Registrar about the
         occurrence of the event.

8. Keeper or the owner of a place set apart for the disposal of the dead bodies or any person
         required by a local authority to be present at such place are required to notify the
         concerned Registrar about the occurrence of the death.

9. The Registrar(s) of Births and Deaths have the power under Section 21 of the Act to obtain
         information regarding birth or death from any person to furnish any information within
         her/his knowledge.

10. Births and deaths are to be reported within 21 days of occurrence of the event.

11. The birth and death certificates are issued free of charge by the concerned Registrar for
         events reported within 21 days.

12. The birth/death reported after 21 days but within 30 days can be registered on payment
         of the prescribed fee.

13. The birth/death reported after 30 days but within one year of its occurrence can be
         registered with the written permission of the prescribed authority and on production of an
         affidavit made before a notary public or any other officer authorised by the State
         Government and on payment of the fee as prescribed.

14. Births and deaths reported after one year of its occurrence shall be registered only on an
         order of the Magistrate of the First Class after verifying the correctness and on payment of
         the prescribed fee.

                                                                             x
15. A birth can be registered without the name of the child.
16. Name of the child can be entered in the birth certificate within 12 months without any fee

         and after one year but within 15 years of registration on payment of prescribed fee and by
         approaching the concerned Registrar.
17. Error(s), if any, in the particulars of birth or death records can be corrected as per the
         prescribed procedure under Section 15 of the Act.
18. Any entry in the birth or death records made fraudulently or improperly, can be cancelled
         by the concerned Registrar.
19. Under Section 17 of the RBD Act, 1969, any person can cause a search in the birth or death
         registers for any entry and obtain a certified extract. Such extracts shall be admissible as
         evidence of birth or death to which the entry relates.
20. All Registrars are required to send a monthly return to the concerned Addl. District
         Registrar/District Registrar or Chief Registrar.
21. The birth of an Indian Citizen, occurred outside the country, can also be registered in India
         within sixty days of return of the parents at the place where they intend to settle. After this
         period, it could be registered under delayed registration provisions of the Act.
22. There is no provision to register death of an Indian Citizen that has occurred outside the
         country.
23. Non-reporting of births and deaths, giving false information for inclusion in the register,
         refusing to put signature in the reporting form/register and non-registration of events
         reported to the Registrar is punishable under the Act.
24. Under the RBD Act, 1969, Registrars and Sub-Registrars are the public servants.
25. Registration of births and deaths made prior to implementation of the RBD Act, 1969 is
         deemed to have been done under the said Act.
26. On adoption of a child, the name of the adoptive parents and a new name for the child can
         be entered in the birth register.

                                                         

                                                                             xi
                                      EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

         Civil Registration System (CRS) in India is the unified process of continuous, permanent,
compulsory, and universal recording of vital events (births, deaths, stillbirths) and characteristics
thereof. The Registration of Births and Deaths Act, 1969 (Act No. 18 of 1969) provides for the
compulsory registration of births and deaths. Civil Registration records are the best source of Vital
Statistics. For the individual, records emanating from CRS provides her/his legal identity and
access to the rights of a citizen including entitlements (social benefits provided by the
Government). For the country, the requirement of a complete Civil Registration System is a must
as it has important administrative and statistical uses. The data generated through a complete and
up to date CRS is essential for socio-economic planning and to evaluate the effectiveness of various
social sector programmes. They also serve as the cornerstone of the public health system. The
Annual Report on Vital Statistics of India based on Civil Registration System for 2022 has been
brought out on the basis of reports provided by the Chief Registrar of Births and Deaths of
respective States/Union Territories. The main features of the Annual Report are summarized
below:

Birth Registration:

    · The number of registered births has increased to 254.4 lakhs in 2022 from 242.0 lakhs in 2021.
         (Statement 8, page 33; Chart 1, page 34)

    · Out of the total registered births, the share of males and females are 52.4% and 47.6%
         respectively. (Statement 15, page 43; Chart 6, page 43).

    · The share of births registered in rural and urban was 43.5% and 56.5% respectively.
         (Statement 17, page 45; Chart 10 page 46).

    · The share of institutional births to total registered births is 75.5 %. However, this does not
         include information from Sikkim (Chart 14, page 49).

    · In case of registration of births within the prescribed time period of 21 days, 13 States/UTs
         namely Puducherry, Gujarat, Lakshadweep, Chandigarh, A & N Islands, D & N Haveli and Daman
         & Diu, Tamil Nadu, Haryana, Himachal Pradesh, Punjab, Goa, Odisha and Andhra Pradesh have
         achieved more than 90% registration of births to the total births registered. (Statement 12,
         page 38)

    · 4 States i.e. Maharashtra, Mizoram, Chhattisgarh and Madhya Pradesh have achieved more than
         80 percent to less than or equal to 90 percent registration of births within 21 days. (Statement
         12, page 38)

    · Further, 14 States/UTs viz. Tripura, Assam, Telangana, West Bengal, Kerala, Bihar, Jharkhand,
         Ladakh, Uttar Pradesh, Karnataka, Rajasthan, Meghalaya, Delhi and Jammu & Kashmir are in

                                                                            xii
         the category of more than 50 percent to less than or equal to 80 percent in registering within
         the stipulated time period of 21 days and the remaining 4 States namely Uttarakhand, Manipur,
         Arunachal Pradesh and Nagaland are under the category of less than or equal to 50 percent in
         completing the birth registration within the time limit of 21 days.(Statement 12, page 38)
    · Highest Sex Ratio at Birth (SRB) based on Registered events has been reported by Nagaland
         (1068), followed by Arunachal Pradesh (1036), Ladakh (1027), Meghalaya (972), and Kerala
         (971). (Statement 22, Page 51 Chart 16, page 52)
    · Lowest SRB has been reported by Bihar (891) followed by Maharashtra (906) and Telangana
         (907). (Statement 22, Page 51; Chart 16, page 52)

 Still Birth Registration:

    · The number of registered still births has decreased from 1.24 lakhs in 2021 to 1.15 lakhs in
         2022 (Statement 21, page 50)

    · Share of registered still births in rural and urban area is 41.5% and 58.5% respectively
         (Statement 21, page 50).

Death Registration:

    · The number of registered deaths has decreased from 102.2 lakhs in 2021 to 86.5 lakhs in 2022.
         (Statement 8, page 33; Chart 2, page 34)

    · Out of the total registered deaths, the share of male and female are 60.4% and 39.6%
         respectively. (Statement 15, page 43; Chart 7, page 43).

    · The share of deaths registered in rural and urban was 59.5% and 40.5% respectively.
         (Statement 17, page 45; Chart 11 page 46).

    · The share of Institutional deaths in total registered deaths is 25.0%. However, this does not
         include information from Sikkim (Chart 15, page 49).

    · In case of registration of deaths within the prescribed time period of 21 days, 9 States/UTs
         namely Chandigarh, Punjab, Lakshadweep, Haryana, Mizoram, Puducherry, D & N Haveli and
         Daman & Diu, Himachal Pradesh, and A & N Islands have achieved more than 90% registration
         of deaths to the total deaths registered. (Statement 12, page 38)

    · 9 States/UTs i.e. Andhra Pradesh, Meghalaya, Maharashtra, Tamil Nadu, Gujarat, Goa,
         Chhattisgarh, Madhya Pradesh, and Telangana have achieved More than 80 percent to less than
         or equal to 90 percent registration of deaths within 21 days. (Statement 12, page 38)

    · Further, 12 States viz. Odisha, West Bengal, Tripura, Delhi, Rajasthan, Assam, Uttar Pradesh,
         Bihar, Karnataka, Uttarakhand, Jharkhand, and Ladakh are in the category of More than 50
         percent to less than 80 percent in registering within the stipulated time period of 21 days and

                                                                            xiii
    remaining 5 states namely Jammu & Kashmir, Manipur, Kerala, Nagaland, and Arunachal
    Pradesh are under the category of less than or equal to 50 percent in completing the birth
    registration within the time limit of 21 days. (Statement 12, page 38)
· In the case of registration of infant deaths, the share of the urban area is 81.5% compared to
    rural with only 18.5%. (Statement 19, page 49).

                                      GLOSSARY OF TERMS

Birth: Live birth or still birth.
Live birth: Complete expulsion or extraction from its mother of a product of conception, irrespective of the
duration of pregnancy, which, after such expulsion or extraction, breathes or shows any other evidence of
life, and each product of such birth is considered live-born.
Still Birth: Foetal death where a product of conception has attained at least the prescribed period of
gestation.
Death: Permanent disappearance of all evidence of the life at any time after live birth has taken place.
Infant Death: Death of a child less than one year old.
Level of reporting: indicates the status of returns of registered births and deaths by the concerned
Registrar within a State / UT during the year.
Sex Ratio of Registered Birth:

                                                                       
                        =         × 1000

Delayed Registered Birth events of more than one year have been duly deducted for arriving the sex ratio
of Registered Birth
Gross Natural addition to the Population = Total Registered Births ­ Total Registered Deaths

                                                    CHAPTER ­ I

                          CIVIL REGISTRATION SYSTEM - AN INTRODUCTION

1.1 The requirement for a complete and up to date Vital Statistical System to yield reliable
data on vital events hardly needs any emphasis. These data are essential for socio-economic
planning and development and also to evaluate the effective implementation of various public
schemes and programs. The main sources of data on vital events in India are Civil Registration
System (CRS), Sample Registration System (SRS) and Population Census. Though the Population
Census is the main source of information on population and its characteristics; however, being
a decennial exercise, it does not provide the measure of changes in population from year to year.
The measures of fertility and mortality derived from Census are centered on the midpoint of the
decade and as such do not provide yearly change in the population. Of the other two sources of
vital statistics for getting the vital rates on a regular basis, the SRS has been designed to provide
reliable estimates at National and State level. Robust estimates of vital rates at District level on
an annual basis through sample survey are difficult to obtain on account of prohibitively large
sample size and variety of resultant factors such as controlling of non-sampling errors etc. CRS
is thus the only source for providing vital rates at district level. Rather, a complete CRS can
provide these rates at lower administrative levels.

1.2 The Civil Registration System (CRS) may be defined as a unified process of continuous,
permanent, compulsory and universal recording of the vital events and characteristics thereof,
as per legal requirements in the country. In India, the Civil Registration System covers
registration of births and deaths. Civil registration system provides the best source of
information on the vital rates at all levels. Ensuring cent percent coverage of vital events by
registration machinery can help in getting the critical vital statistics at all administrative levels,
equally important for planning and research. Therefore, emphasis should also be given to the
proper monitoring of the activities under the system. Civil registration system is considered to
be complete if it covers 100 percent births and deaths taking place within a country or area and
birth and death certificates are issued to all registered events.

1.3 The registration records are primarily useful for their value as legal documents and
secondarily as the source of vital statistics. Civil registration as a source of vital statistics is
relatively less expensive, as the statistics generated from the registration records are the by-
products of an essential administrative process. The registration records can also generate
current and continuous statistics, which can be more accurate than any other method of data
collection apart from being a continuous source of such data. However, the accuracy of the vital
statistics obtained through the registration system depends much on the completeness and
promptness with which events are registered and the correctness of the information in the
registration records. The improvement in the system of registration of births and deaths is
critically dependent on the importance given by the respective States and District
administrations to this work and regular monitoring by them. Besides, the level of awareness

                                                                            3
among the general public about the utility of birth or death certificate is also equally and crucially
important.

1.4 Vital statistics obtained through registration records at different administrative levels
enable estimation of the size, structure and geographical distribution of the population except
for migration. Vital statistics also enables the projections for coming years on the basis of
probable trends of fertility and mortality as derived from these vital statistics and their
interactions with social, economic and other demographic factors. The vital statistics data
generated through an effective Civil Registration System is of immense use for formulation of
various development and welfare programmes/ schemes and increases their effectiveness at
State and District level. The Civil Registration data acquires paramount importance in the wake
of 73rd and 74th amendments to the constitution of India, as it can provide data at local level for
micro level planning, monitoring and evaluation of schemes. At national level, the vital statistics
data generated through Civil Registration is also quite useful for medical research and in the
study of sex ratio, mortality rates and also in the study of causes of deaths.

Registration of births and deaths in India

1.5 In India, the registration of births and deaths is carried out under provisions of the
Registration of Births and Deaths (RBD) Act, 1969, a central Act implemented by State/UT
Governments. The registration is done by the Registrar of the local area, appointed by concerned
State/UT Government.

1.6 Provision for registration of births and deaths and the mechanism thereof had been in
existence in India for more than a century. Earlier, the work of registration of birth and death
was carried out under various laws and byelaws existing at that time in different parts of the
country. The need for improvement in the vital statistics system was felt for long and, therefore,
after detailed deliberations at various levels, the Registration of Births & Deaths Act, 1969 was
enacted providing compulsory registration of births and deaths in India. Since then, this Act has
been enforced uniformly across all the States and Union territories (UT). This Act provides for
registration of births and deaths and publication of statistical reports annually based on the civil
registration data. The enactment of this Act has provided a common framework for putting in
place an effective system of registration of births and deaths in the country.

1.7 In order to simplify and speed up the process of registration and to bring in uniformity in
birth and death registration formats, the registration system was revamped in the year 2000.
Under the revamped system, the procedure for registration of births and deaths was also
modified to some extent and the forms were redesigned. The revamped system became
operational in 2000 in all States/Union Territories except Assam, Arunachal Pradesh, Gujarat,
Haryana, Himachal Pradesh, Jharkhand, Odisha, Punjab, Uttarakhand, and Uttar Pradesh. It was
implemented in Assam, Arunachal Pradesh, and Odisha in 2001, Haryana in 2002, Himachal

                                                                            4
Pradesh, Uttarakhand, and Uttar Pradesh in 2003, Gujarat and Punjab in 2004, and Jharkhand in
2009.

Statistical Reports
1.8 System of registration of births and deaths is constantly generating statistical
information at the local level. The information contained in the reporting forms is consolidated
by the Registrar for onward transmission to the Chief Registrar of Births & Deaths or to any
officer specified in the concerned State/UT. The Chief Registrar of the States/UTs, who are the
chief executive authorities for the activities on registration of Births and Deaths in the State/UT,
are mandated to submit to the State Government Annual Report on the Working of the Act in the
State/UT along with the Statistical Report referred under section 19(2) on registered births and
deaths for each year immediately following the year to which the report relates to. The Chief
Registrars are also required to send a copy of these Reports to the Registrar General, India, Govt.
of India.
1.9 Based on the Annual Reports prepared by the States/UTs, the Office of the Registrar
General, India, brings out an annual comprehensive report entitled "Vital Statistics of India based
on Civil Registration System". These reports have been brought out since 1958 (Before the
enactment of the Act, only statistical reports were brought out). However, some of the States
are not bringing out their Annual Statistical Reports in time, and this in turn, delays the
publication of the corresponding national report. It has also been observed that some States also
do not include all the prescribed tables in their reports thereby creating a limitation while
bringing out the national report. However, notwithstanding all these constraints, this report
provides vital indicators including the District-wise registered births and deaths along with
infant deaths with the sex-wise and rural-urban break-up of registered events for the year 2022.

                                                        

                                                                            5
              CHAPTER-II

             ANNUAL REPORT FOR 2022 ON THE WORKING OF
     THE REGISTRATION OF BIRTHS AND DEATHS (RBD) ACT, 1969

2.1. Annual report on the working of the Registration of Births and Deaths (RBD)
Act, 1969: Under the provision of Section 4 (4) of the Act, the Chief Registrars are required
to submit an Annual Report on the working of the RBD Act to the respective State
Governments and after approval a copy of same is sent to Office of Registrar General of
India (ORGI) . The ORGI consolidate/compile the reports received from States/UTs and
release the working of RBD Act report at National level. The aforesaid statutory Annual
Report for the year 2022 has been received from all the States/UTs.
2.2. Registration Machinery: Section 3 to 7 of the Registration of Births and Deaths
(RBD)Act, 1969 provides for the appointment of Civil Registration System functionaries at
all administrative and Jurisdictional levels like: -

(i)  National level:- Registrar General, India; section 3 of the RBD Act

(ii) State level:- Chief Registrar; section 4 of the Act

(iii) District level: - District Registrar/Additional District Registrar under Section 6 of

     the Act

(iv) Local area level: - Registrar for a village, Panchayat, municipality, municipal

     Corporation or other local bodies; under Section 7(1) of the Act and

(v) Sub-Registrar for specified area within the area under the jurisdiction of a

     Registrar under section 7 (5) of the Act.

           The Central Government under section 3(1) of the RBD Act, 1969 appoints the
Registrar General, India (RGI) at Central level who has been empowered under the said Act
to take steps to coordinate and unify the activities of Chief Registrars of all States/Union
Territories and issues general directions regarding registration of births and deaths under
section 3(3) of the Act. The work relating to Civil Registration in the office of the Registrar
General, India is handled in the Vital Statistics Division.

           The State Governments under section 4 (1) of the Act appoints Chief Registrar for
the State/UT. The Chief Registrars under section 4 (3) of the said Act has been declared as
the chief executive authority in the respective States/ UTs for implementing the provisions
of RBD Act and the Rules and orders made thereunder. The State Government also
appoints District Registrar of births and deaths for each revenue district under section 6
of the RBD Act. The actual work of registration of births and deaths in the field is carried
out by the local Registrars, who are appointed under section 7 of the said Act.

           There is a wide variation in the registration machinery across the country, details
of which are given below: -

     The Chief Registrars of births and deaths in 17 States and 4 Union Territories are

from the Health Department, in 10 States and 2 Union Territories are from Planning,

Economics & Statistics Department, in 1 State (Kerala) from Panchayat Department, in UT

              6
of Dadra & Nagar Haveli & Daman & Diu, Chief Secretary/Finance Secretary Office, in
remaining 1 UT (Puducherry) it is with Local Administration Department.

  The district level authorities in the States/UTs such as District Collectors/ District

Magistrate, Additional Collector, Chief Medical Officers/ District Medical and Health, Civil

Surgeon, District Statistical/ Planning Officers, Deputy Commissioner, Deputy Director,

Panchayat and Dy. Director, local administration are appointed as District Registrar.

  At Rural level, the registration work is entrusted to the officials of Panchayat

Department, Health Department, Revenue Department and Education Department in rural

areas of States/UTs. In urban areas, the Health Officer of Municipal Corporation /

Municipalities etc. or in absence of such a post, the Executive Officer of the town

administration functions as the Registrar of Births and Deaths (State and UT wise detail of

registration functionaries is attached at Annexure-A).

2.3. Registration of Births and Deaths occurring to Indian Citizens abroad:
Under the provisions of Citizenship Act, 1955 the event of births and deaths of Indian
Citizens occurring abroad are registered by the respective Indian Missions/Consulates.
Under Rule 5 of Citizen's (Registration at Indian Consulates) Rules 1956, Consulates are
statutorily required to send the annual returns of births and deaths of Indian Citizens to
the office of the RGI. It has been observed that requisite returns are not furnished by all
Indian Missions/Consulates; such returns for the year 2022 have been received from 183
missions located in 148 countries, covering 44315 births and 9146 deaths. As can be seen
from the Statement 2, Out of total registered births at the various missions during 2022,
Mission/Consulate of Abu Dhabi located in UAE registered 4430 i.e. about 10 per cent of
the Total births registered abroad, followed by 9.7 per cent by Mission/Consulate of
London. In case of Deaths, Mission/Consulate of Dubai located in UAE registered 1750 i.e.
19.1 per cent of the total deaths registered abroad followed by 9.8 per cent by
Mission/Consulate of Jeddah located in Saudi Arabia.

2.1. Inter Departmental Coordination Committees (IDCC): In order to resolve the
operational problems and for proper implementation of the provisions of the RBD Act and
corresponding State Rules, coordination among the implementing departments is
essentially required. On the other hand, the work of registration of births and deaths in
most of the States/UTs is being done through the officials drawn from more than one
department of the State Government/Union Territory Administration. The need for
coordination between the stake holder departments is a pre-condition for effective
functioning of the system in each of the State. In view of the above, the State level Inter-
Departmental Coordination Committees (IDCCs) have been constituted in States and
Union Territories. In most of the States / UTs, IDCC committee is chaired by the Principal
Secretary/Secretary of the Department from which the Chief Registrar belongs.

           In order to formulate the strategies for further improvements in Civil Registration
System and effective implementation of the provisions of the RBD Act in the respective
State, it is desired to convene the meeting of such committee at least once in a year.
However, it has been observed that despite persuasion of this office with the State
Governments from time to time, such meetings have not been convened regularly by most
of the States/ UTs.

           During 2022, such meetings were held in 23 States/UTs namely Arunachal
Pradesh, Assam, Bihar, Chhattisgarh, Goa, Gujarat, Himachal Pradesh, Jharkhand,

                8
Karnataka, Kerala, Madhya Pradesh, Meghalaya, Mizoram, Nagaland, Odisha, Punjab,
Rajasthan, Tamil Nadu, Tripura, Uttar Pradesh, Uttarakhand, West Bengal and Chandigarh.

          Such Coordination Committees have also been set up at District and below district
level (i.e. at Mandal and Taluka level) in many States. However, the information on
convening the regular meeting of District Level Coordination Committees (DLCCs) and
below district level meetings has not been received from all the States/UTs. The following
information regarding district level and below district level meetings during 2022 has
been provided by the Chief Registrars in the respective annual report of State/UT:-

       Arunachal Pradesh and Goa: DLCC meetings were not conducted in the year

2022.

       Bihar: Various meetings at State, district and block level were held to review the

progress of vital Statistics and to discuss the difficulties encountered during online

registration of birth and death.

       Chhattisgarh: 10 DLCC meetings were conducted during the year 2022.

       Gujarat: During 2022, monthly meetings of registration officials were organized

at the Taluka level, in the first week of every month by Taluka registrar. Meeting of Taluka

Statistical Assistants were also organized at district HQ every month for collecting the

compiled information of monthly returns. One person from State HQ attended such

meetings. DLCC has been constituted under the Chairmanship of the District Collector and

District Magistrate. State level IDCC meeting has been done under the chairmanship of

Chief Registrar (Birth and Death) to smoothen the registration process and co-ordinate the

activities of the registration of births and deaths in the State.

       Haryana: A meeting of registration officials were organized at State H.Q. on

12.05.2022.

       Jharkhand: 14 District level and 29 Block level Coordination Committee meetings

were convened during the year.

       Karnataka: 39 District level and 420 Taluk level Coordination Committee

meetings were convened during the year 2022.

       Madhya Pradesh: 14 DLCC meetings were held in the year 2022.

       Maharashtra: DLCC meetings were held in the year 2022 in 4 districts namely

Nandurbar, Thane, Ratnagiri and Buldhana. In addition, reporting and registration

activities were discussed in periodical meeting held via video conferencing conducted by

higher officials.

                                  9
       Meghalaya: DLCC meetings were held in three districts namely, East Khasi Hills,

East Jaintia Hills, and North Garo Hills during the year 2022.

       Mizoram: DLCC meetings were convened in three districts namely Champai,

Siaha and Lunglei in the year 2022.

       Odisha: 12 DLCC meetings were held in the year 2022.

       Punjab:, 27 DLCC meetings were held in 18 Districts during the year 2022.

       Rajasthan: 7 DLCC and 79 Block Level Coordination Committee meetings were

held during the year 2022.

       Tamil Nadu: DLCC meetings were conducted in all districts under Chairmanship

of District Collectors and District Revenue Officers and District Registrars of births and

deaths during 2022.

       Tripura: Monthly meetings with the registration authority of different medical

Institutions were held during the year 2022.

       Uttarakhand: Two State level meetings were held in the Year 2022. DLCC

meetings were also held in all the districts during the year 2022.

       West Bengal: Two DLCC meetings were arranged during the year 2022.

       Andaman & Nicobar Islands: The registration of Births and Deaths in the Island

is solely carried out by the Health Department except one Defence Hospital which is solely

monitored by the O/o the CRBD which ensure better co-ordination and effective

functioning among Civil Registration functionaries. No IDCC meeting was held during

2022.

       Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu: Due to Administrative reasons IDCC

meeting was not conducted during the year.

2.2. Computerization of Civil registration record/ work: The detail of information
furnished by States/ UTs on computerization of civil registration records and online
registration is given below: -

       Andhra Pradesh: Uniform software developed by Office of Registrar General

India (ORGI), New Delhi (www.crsorgi.gov.in) is being implemented in all the Birth and

Death registration units i.e. Gram Panchayats, Govt. Health Facilities and Municipalities

from 01.11.2015, 01.05.2016 & 01.12.2016 respectively in all the Registration units. After

implementation of software the mechanism of Registration of Birth & Death and issue of

Certificates is very simplified for Registrars as well as public. Most of the ULBs have

                                              10
completed (>90%) the digitalization of old records from 2005 to 2016.Remaining all old
records of all registration units shall be digitized at the earliest.

  Arunachal Pradesh: All district headquarters have been equipped with

computers and initiated the procedure of computerization for record of births and deaths.

In the State online registration facility is available in 20 District Headquarters namely

Bomdila, Yupia, Ziro, Aalo, Koloriang, Yingkiong, Pasighat, Boleng, Tezu, Roing, Hawai,

Seppa, Daporijo, Changlang, Longding, Tawang, Palin, Khonsa, Namsai and Anini

registration units. 41 ADCs (HQs) namely, Mio, Jairampur, Mechuka, Seijusa Gepen,

Yomcha Kamba, Nari, Kamba, Yomcha Rupa, Nafra, Singchung, Thrizino, Kalaktang, Dirang,

Tali, Pania, Nyapin, Patuk, Rumgong, Chongkam, Kanubari, Siyum, Taliha, Dumporijo,

Kimin, Sagalee, Balijan, Lumla, Jang, Mariyang, Tuting, Deomali, Yachuli, Dambuk, Wakro,

Mebo, Ruksin, Bameng, Chayangtajo, Jairampur, Bordumsa, Mio, Bordumsa and Hayuliang

registration units. Provision for registration facilities in other registration units is offline.

  Assam: Uniform software developed by Office of Registrar General India (ORGI),

New Delhi (www.crsorgi.gov.in) has been implemented in urban units including Medical

Colleges and Hospitals, District Civil Hospitals and Municipal Corporation / Boards in all

the districts. 409 numbers of registration units had been covered with CRS online

application.

  Bihar: The registration of births and deaths is being done through ORGI uniform

CRS software (www.crsorgi.gov.in). 30,71,603 births and 4,58,133 deaths were registered

online during 2022 in the State.

  Chhattisgarh: In the State, 15736 Registration Units are registering events online

through ORGI uniform CRS software (www.crsorgi.gov.in). Remaining Registration units

are registering events manually and the Statistical parts of the reporting forms are entered

on software at district level to prepare analytical reports. The old records before the year

2020 are yet to be digitized.

  Goa: All birth and death records in the State are computerized. The system is

functioning smoothly, effectively and efficiently. More than 13 lakh births and 5 lakh plus

death events have been registered since the implementation of the RBD Act, 1969 in the

State. During the year 2022, 18065 birth events and 15139 death events have been

registered under the RBD Act, 1969.

  Gujarat: A web-based application namely "e-Olakh" developed by State NIC is

being used in the State for the purpose of registration of birth and death. All Gram

Panchayats, municipalities and 8 municipal corporations have been covered under "e-

Olakh" (GCRS). "e-olakh" application is in operation since 2015.

  Haryana: In the State, the registration of birth and death is being done only online

using ORGI uniform CRS portal (www.crsorgi.gov.in) and computerized birth and death

certificate are being issued by all the Registration Units. The Aadhaar linkage of new born

to the birth registration is also started in the State. For online registration and Aadhaar

                                     11
linkage about 481 Laptop and 540 Tablets were provided to the Registration Units. Efforts
have been made to link the birth and death registration to ensure its regular updation. Few
services such as application for issuance of birth/death certificates/Non-Availability
Certificate(NAC), correction in records, inclusion of child name and delayed registration
are being delivered in online mode through Antyodaya Saral Portal developed and
maintained under the Chief Minister Good Governance Programme (CMGGA).

         Himachal Pradesh: The registration of birth and death is being done using ORGI

uniform CRS portal (www.crsorgi.gov.in). "90988" birth, "49959" death & "847" Infant

Deaths have been registered through CRS Portal during the year 2022.

         Jharkhand: The registration of birth and death is being done using ORGI uniform

CRS portal (www.crsorgi.gov.in) during 2022. "702144" births and "146318" deaths were

registered online during 2022 in the State.

         Karnataka: Registration of birth and death events is being carried out online in

the State through uniform web-based application namely e-JanMa developed by State NIC

since 2018.

         Kerala:

  · Civil Registration has been computerized in all the registration units by using the

softwares developed by Information Kerala Mission. To register a birth or death in Grama

Panchayats, the Integrated Local Governance Management Software system and in urban

units `Sevana' Civil Registration Software is being used. The Civil Registration records

prior to the date of computerization have been digitized. The certificates in respect of the

registers maintained under the traditional system are also issued using the software in

computerized units.

  · Under the Hospital Kiosk Project, online registration of birth and death events

occurring in hospitals has been implemented in 6 Corporations, 87 Municipalities and

227 Gram Panchayats up to the end of 2022. The process of implementation of the Kiosk

project in more hospitals is in progress.

  · Under this Project, the report of births and deaths are sent to the Registration Unit

as electronic data through telephone line from the kiosks, which are functioning in the

hospital. The electronic report received at the Registration Unit is registered after

verification and the certificate under Section 12 is issued through the hospital. Efforts are

made to issue the birth certificate before the mother and baby leaves the hospital.

         Madhya Pradesh: Online registration of births and deaths has been initiated from

01-01-2015 in the State of Madhya Pradesh using ORGI uniform CRS portal

(www.crsorgi.gov.in). During 2022, out of total registered birth (1789365) and death

(511168), 99.93% (1788220) births and 99.99% (511167) deaths have been registered

online.

                                             12
  Maharashtra: In the urban and rural areas of the State, the ORGI uniform CRS

portal (www.crsorgi.gov.in) is being used for registration of birth and death events from

2016 onwards.

  Manipur : No computerization of civil registration record took place during the

year 2022. Out of 320 registration units (RUs), 108 RUs have started using online CRS

portal in the year 2022.

  Meghalaya: Online registration process has been implemented in some

Registration Units namely Ganesh Das Maternal and child health hospital, Shillong, Civil

hospital Shillong, Shillong Municipal board, Tura Municipal board, Tura Civil hospital,

North Eastern Indira Gandhi Regional Institute of Health & Medical Sciences, R.P. Chest

Hospital. Computerization of old Civil Registration Records has not been possible due to

lack of fund.

  Mizoram: Digitization of all the Civil Registration Records in Mizoram was

completed during 2019. These digitized records contain some minor errors and the same

are being rectified. Online Registration is not yet done at the Registration Unit level.

However, online submission to ORGI's CRS portal of all the reports of live Births, still births

and deaths are done at the respective ADR Offices. Moreover, all the records are also kept

in digitized form in the State specific software 'CRS Mizoram'.

  Nagaland: Births & Deaths data is compiled through computerization in every

district headquarter. Currently the Registration units in the village/block level are

functioning manually only. Online registration at district level not functional due to

connectivity issues. The State is taking concrete steps to upgrade the ICT capability of the

district headquarters for implementation of the ORGI uniform CRS portal

(www.crsorgi.gov.in) for online registration.

  Odisha: Online registration of births and deaths in 116 urban registration units

and all 314 Rural registration units started from 01.01.2021 in OBDRS portal, developed

by the State, and is continuing till date.

  Punjab: The e-Sewa application was introduced by the Department of

Governance Reforms (DoGR), Punjab in July, 2019 after revamping the earlier e-District

application. The services of issuance of birth and death certificate, addition of name of

child, correction in record and delayed registration of birth and death have been included

in the e-Sewa application and 535 Sewa Kendra's were established in the State.

  Rajasthan: The online registration of births and deaths through the web portal

`Pehchan' is being done since 01.01.2014 in the State. 100 percent computerization and

online registration is going on in the State.

                                               13
  Sikkim: In the State, out of total 33 registration units, 13 are presently doing

online registration on CRS portal of ORGI.

  Tamil Nadu: The common CRS software (www.crstn.org) is implemented

uniformly throughout the State from 2018 onwards for registration of birth and death and

to issue/download the birth and death certificates. The Government have ordered vide

G.O.(D) No.1358 Health and Family Welfare (AB2) Department dated 21.12.2022 for

implementation of e-Mortality software of ICMR-NCDIR integrated with CRS software for

capturing the cause of death across the state in all Government, Private Medical

Institutions and all Home Deaths which are medically certified towards strengthening

Medical Certification of Cause of Death.

  Telangana:, Civil registration records are fully digitalized for the year 2022.

  Tripura: The work of online registration of births & deaths and reporting &

monitoring of each event has been started through ORGI uniform CRS software

(www.crsorgi.gov.in) all over the State from 2016 onwards.

  Uttarakhand: The online registration of births & deaths is being done in almost

all urban units in the State through ORGI uniform CRS software (www.crsorgi.gov.in). The

same CRS software is being used in a few rural Registration Units also, while maximum

number of units are using e-district portal/Apuni Sarkar Portal in rural areas.

  Uttar Pradesh: The registration of birth and death is being done using ORGI

uniform CRS portal (www.crsorgi.gov.in). During the year 2022, "5443003" births,

"1223176" deaths have been registered.

  West Bengal: The State's own portal Janma Mrityu Tathya (JMT) has been used

since 5th May, 2022 and the use of ORGI Portal has been discontinued. CRS Portal & JMT

Portal have been integrated and almost all data registered in CRS Portal are available as old

legacy data in JMT Portal. Out of "1358851" births and "584587" deaths, "1356130" births,

and "581966" deaths have been registered using the State's own Software.

  Andaman & Nicobar Islands: The ORGI uniform CRS online software

(www.crsorgi.gov.in) has been implemented in all the registration units w.e.f. 01-01-2016

and all the old birth and death records have been uploaded in CRS portal w.e.f. Aug 1982.

  Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu: Registration of birth and deaths in all

the registration Centers in Rural as well as Urban area have been computerized and online

registration is being carried out.

  Delhi: All local bodies have computerized their birth and death registration

system and all events are reported online to the concerned local body. All Hospitals,

government/private have ben provided the facility of online reporting of events to speed

up the process of reporting /registration of events. Further, online facility to take

birth/death certificates directly from website has also been provided by all local bodies i.e

                                            14
. New Delhi Municipal Council (NDMC), Municipal Corporation of Delhi (MCD) and Delhi
Cantonment Board (DCB).

  Jammu & Kashmir: 89 Registration centers of Urban/Rural areas have

reportedly been computerized their civil Registration records of which, 76, located in

Urban area, belong to Housing & Urban Development Department while remaining 11

located in rural areas are of Home Department. Till the Calendar Year 2021, about

2114109 birth records, 385232 death records and 05 Still Birth records had been digitized

  Ladakh: The online Registration system CRS started in UT ­ Ladakh from

December, 2022 onwards.

  Lakshadweep: All the registration units of Hospital/CHC/PHC are computerized

with support of UT of Lakshadweep Administration from 2008 onwards. Now all Births,

Deaths & still births are registered online by using the CRS uniform software provided by

the ORGI and therefore, online certificates are issued on time. The department is planning

to digitalize the births, deaths & still births records prior to 2014 in order to facilitate the

general public.

  Puducherry: All local bodies are issuing computerized birth and death

certificates for the event registered under the Act. In order to cater the needs of the public

who requires birth and death certificates of their ancestors, computerization of birth and

death record has been taken up for the period prior to 1979 in Puducherry and Oulgaret

Municipalities. The computerized birth records from 1930 and death records from 1954

are available in Puducherry Municipality. In Oulgaret Municipality birth records from 1920

and death records from 1979 are available.

2.3. Penalties: There is a provision for imposing penalties on defaulting institutions,
Registrars and the individuals for non-registration, non-reporting and negligence or
refusal to register any birth or death without any reasonable cause under section 23 of the
RBD Act, 1969. Despite the aforesaid provision, under the Act and Rule, most of the State
Governments are not imposing penalties on the defaulters as a means to improve the
functioning of the system. It has been found that very few States have reported the details
on imposing the penalties. The details of imposing penalties and prosecutions were not
provided by majority of States. However, few States have initiated several actions in this
regard, the details of which are given below: -

  Andhra Pradesh: Show cause notices were issued to the Registrars by the District

Registrars, where Birth and Death events were registered fraudulently without proper

field verification. All the DM & Health Officers-(HOs-District Registrars) and the Municipal

Commissioners / Municipal Health Officers (Urban Registrars) were authorized to launch

prosecutions and compounding of offences and they were instructed to exercise their

powers of improvement of Registration under section 23 of the Registration of Births and

Deaths Act 1969.

                                            15
         Chhattisgarh: Only one penalty was imposed in the Year 2022.

         Kerala: During 2022, the maximum numbers of prosecutions (137) were from

Manjeri Urban Municipality followed by Palakkad panchayat (66). The maximum number

of offences 53, 46 and 31 compounded in Kannur, Malappuram and Kasaragod Panchayats

respectively. Details are given below:

                  Statement 4: Details of Penalties imposed by States

                                                prosecutions

         Rajasthan: Penalties and offences were laid on 41 Registrar / Sub-Registrar

during the year 2022 under Section 23 of RBD Act, 1969.

         Tamil Nadu: Directions have been issued under Section 4(4) to the connected

heads of departments to instruct the Birth and Death Registrars to ensure that the section

23 of the Registration of Births and Deaths Act is enforced to improve the registration

system. During inspection by the Supervisory Officials the above provision was sensitized

and guided for registration of the events.

         Telangana: All the DM & HOs (District Registrars) and the Municipal

Commissioners / Municipal Health Officers (Urban Registrars) were authorized to launch

prosecutions and compounding of offences and they were instructed to exercise their

powers for improvement of Registration under section 23 of the Registration of Births and

Deaths Act 1969.

         Uttarakhand: No incident of prosecution and compounding of offences has taken

place in the State. Improvement has been made by giving adequate publicity to educate

and sensitize general public through appeal to stay away from wrong doing and legal

tangle.

         Chandigarh: In the year 2022, the penalties were imposed under section 23 of

the RBD Act, 1969 on individuals for negligence to report birth or death.

         No Penalty Imposed: The States of Arunachal Pradesh, Assam, Goa, Gujarat,

Haryana, Himachal Pradesh, Jharkhand, Karnataka, Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra,

Manipur, Meghalaya, Mizoram, Nagaland, Odisha, Punjab, Sikkim, Tamil Nadu, Telangana,

Tripura, Uttarakhand, West Bengal, Andaman & Nicobar Islands, Dadra & Nagar Haveli and

Daman & Diu, Delhi, Jammu & Kashmir, Ladakh, Lakshadweep and Puducherry have not

imposed any penalty under section 23 of the RBD Act and sent nil report.

       Bihar and Uttar Pradesh: The States of Bihar and Uttar Pradesh have not

provided any information in the report on imposing penalties for non-registration, non-

reporting and for negligence or refusal to register the event.

2.4. Inspection of Registration units: Section 18 of the RBD Act, 1969 provides for
inspection of registration offices and records in order to improve the registration system
in qualitative as well as quantitative terms. For effective civil registration system, reliable
inspection of registration units and registration records at regular interval is required. The
inspections are required to be done by the office of the Chief Registrar and District
Registrars in each State/UT. Such inspections could have been more meaningful, if
organized and conducted frequently within a regular time frame, however, as per the
information provided by the States, the inspections of registration centers were irregular
and extremely deficient in most of the States/UTs during 2022 also. The actual information
in respect of number of units inspected is not provided by majority of the States /UTs, the
details of information regarding inspection provided by some of the States/ UTs during the
year are given below:

and two urban and rural units were inspected in district Chamba during the year 2022.

registrar and additional district registrar, registration centers were inspected by the

officials of Revenue department also. 2360 inspections of the registration units were done

by the District Statistical Officers of the Directorate and Officers/Officials of the Revenue

Department for the year 2022.

        Kerala: In rural units, the Performance Audit Teams under the control of

Assistant Director of Panchayats conducts inspection during every quarter. The schedule

is prepared in advance and inspection report is submitted to the District Registrar. The

District Registrar will forward the same with specific remarks to the Chief Registrar. The

urban units are to be inspected by the Secretary of the Local Self Government Institutions

(LSGI) concerned and they also submit reports to the Chief Registrar. Efforts are made to

issue instructions to rectify the defects pointed out in the inspection reports.

                               18
       Madhya Pradesh: Regular inspections of registration units as per the provisions

of RBD Act, 1969 were done. During the year 2022, inspections of registration units were

made and 44 Registration Units covering 6 Districts namely Sagar, Ujjain, Shajapur Indore,

Hoshangabad and Jabalpur were inspected. Maximum number of inspections were done

in Hoshangabad (21) followed by Sagar (7) districts of Madhya Pradesh. In case of urban

area, 2 registration units of Shajapur District were also inspected.

       Maharashtra: During the year 2022, State level officers inspected 7 Registration

Units namely RH Murud District Raigad, RH Alandi District Raigad, Aurangabad Medical

Thane (8 RUs), and Satara (5 RUs) by the concerned District level officers. The District

Registrars were directed to undertake inspection activities on regular basis.

       Punjab: During the year 2022, number of inspections of registration centers and

their records were carried out in 20 Districts by the Officers/Staff of Chief Registrar Office,

Directorate of Census Operations and District level Officers for both qualitative and

quantitative improvement of civil registration system.

       Sikkim: Routine inspections of registration units and registration records for

improvement of registration were done by the officials from the Headquarter during the

year.

       Tamil Nadu: Regular inspections of Registration units were done by District

Supervisory officials as per the provisions of RBD Act, 1969. Further the State officials and

officials from the office of the DCO were also made a visit to selected registration units to

monitor the registration activities.

       Tripura: Inspections were performed by Data Processing Assistant from time to

time in year 2022.

       Uttarakhand: Regular Inspections of the Registration Units were made by the

appointed Registration authorities and suggestions and directions were given to resolve

the problem faced by Registrar in registering event. The officer at district level inspected

on an average of 12-15 Registration Units monthly. During the year 2022, total 2716

inspections were conducted.

       West Bengal: During the year 2022, 193 Rural and 14 Urban Registration units

were inspected.

       Chandigarh: Inspections in 6 Government Registration units and 27 Private

Hospitals & Nursing homes were made in the year 2022.

       Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu: During the year 2022, regular visits /

inspection on were carried out on Monthly basis in all three districts.

  Delhi: During the year 2022, two registration zones (NDMC and Civil Lines Zone,

  ORGI and DCO: The inspections have also been made by the Officers/ officials of

the ORGI and DCO offices from time to time. The observations found during the

inspections were forwarded to the respective States/UTs for remedial measures.

2.5. Training of Civil Registration Functionaries- Civil registration functionaries
involved in registration of births and deaths at various levels in most of the States/UTs
belong to different departments. Almost all these functionaries perform registration work
as an additional work along with other work assigned to them by the respective
department. In addition, due to frequent transfer of registration functionaries, there is a
need to impart training at regular intervals on RBD Act, 1969 and registration rules,
procedures and practices being followed in the State/UT. As per the information furnished
by the States, the details of training programs organized in various States during the year
2022 are given below:

  Andhra Pradesh: Virtual trainings were conducted to newly recruited /

promoted / transferred registrars and assisting staff in the entire state.

  Arunachal Pradesh: CRS /MCCD Training Programme was organized at

Directorate of Economics & Statistics (DES) - HQ for official of statistics during 2022.

  Assam: A training on online CRS application among the operator were conducted with

the cooperation of Director Census Operations, Assam.

                                           20
           Bihar: 38 District level and 534 block level training programmes were organized

during the year, 2022.

           Chhattisgarh: 201 training programs on provisions of RBD Act and State rules

framed thereunder were organized for civil registration functionaries at district and block

level.

           Goa: Regular interactions are continuously held with the civil registration

functionaries at the Municipal and Panchayat level along with the special birth and death

registration unit at Goa Medical College, Bambolim, in order to solve any

problem/difficulty faced by them in carrying out their day to day activities.

           Gujarat: During the year 2022, Registrars/Sub-Registrars, Taluka Registrars,

Statistical Assistants, Computer Operators etc. were trained on day to day functioning of

the system as per requirement. In addition this, training had also been imparted to

Statistical Assistants and Programme Assistants (CRS) , hospital users etc. in regular

review meetings conducted at State level.

           Jharkhand: 24 District level and 264 block level training programmes were

organized during the year 2022, These programmes were attended by 1403 and 10560

participants respectively at District and block level.

           Karnataka:, 170 District Statistical Officers conducted training programs in all

the Taluks and in Bruhath Bangalore Mahanagara Palike to improve the Civil Registration

System during the year 2022. Detailed procedures of RBD Act and State Rules,

Clarifications and discrepancies in filling up of reporting forms were also discussed during

training.

           Kerala: CRS training to all local registrars was conducted under the leadership of

Kerala Institute of Local Administration using the funds of the state government during

the year 2022.

           Maharashtra: One training programme was organized at Pune Municipal

Corporation for newly appointed Medical officers as Registrar Births and Deaths in the

State.

           Manipur: 17 training programs were organized by the CRS functionaries on

online registration portal and all the functionaries were trained.

           Mizoram: During the year 2022, annual training of RBD, Act and Rules was

convened at all 11 district headquarters and at the same time general guidelines and

registration procedure including frequently asked questions (FAQ) in Mizo (local) dialect

                                           21
were issued by office of the Additional Chief Registrar and the same was distributed to all
Registration Units.

  Odisha: 4 training programmes were organized in the State during the year 2022.

  Punjab: The training of new officials and re-orientation courses for registration

functionaries are arranged at State headquarters to keep the functionaries well versed

with the latest clarifications and provisions of the Act. The participants are also attending

various courses at the Regional Health Statistics Training Centre SAS Nagar and Health and

Family Welfare Training Centre, Amritsar. The State Institute of Health and Family Welfare

Training, Mohali is also imparting trainings on the subject of registration of birth and

death. Apart from this, Department of Governance Reforms is also providing training to all

the registration functionaries involved in the process of various birth and death related

services under E-Sewa regarding usage of this application

  Rajasthan: one State level, 8 District level and 36 block level training programmes

were organized in the State for civil registration functionaries during the year 2022.

  Tamil Nadu: The Registrars are being apprised on the procedures and latest

directions on birth and death registration during the visit of Supervisory officials to

Registration Units. Training was given on e-mor software and the latest executive

instructions on CRS through Zoom.

  Uttarakhand: Regular training programs on Civil Registration System were

organized in the State at all district level. During the year 2022, 37 training programs were

organized for the Registrars, Notifiers/Informants on online registration and issuance of

certificate through CRS web portal, which was attended by 1463 participants.

  West Bengal: Re-orientation training programmes are being conducted by the

Dy. Chief Registrar of Births & Deaths in the State. 13 training programmes were organized

for DPAs and DEOs during the year 2022.

  Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu: No training programme was

conducted during the year. However, issues and problems faced by the registration

functionaries are resolved on regular basis.

  Jammu & Kashmir: 72 training programmes were organized at district level in

collaboration with O/o Directorate of Census Operations, Jammu & Kashmir for all

registration functionaries and for the staff of health institutions/BDO offices in the UT.

  Ladakh : Training of one Batch each was conducted in both the Districts of the UT

on Civil Registration System.

                                              22
  No Training: 11 States/UTs namely, Haryana, Himachal Pradesh, Madhya

Pradesh, Meghalaya, Nagaland, Sikkim, Telangana, Tripura, Andaman & Nicobar Islands,

Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu, and Puducherry did not organize any training

programme on Civil Registration System during the year 2022.

2.6. Publicity efforts for improving Registration of Births and Deaths: Lack of
awareness about the need and importance of registration among the general public and
lack of procedural knowledge among the registration functionaries are important reasons
for low levels of registration in the country. In order to make the public aware about the
need and importance of registration, the State governments had undertaken various
publicity measures through various means with their own funds like printing of posters,
cloth banners, pamphlets, leaflets on the importance of registration of births and deaths.
Radio talks were also arranged on the importance of registration of births and deaths,
advertisements were published in leading newspapers. As per the details given by the
States, following publicity measures have been taken by the States during the year: -

  Andhra Pradesh: Publicity on Civil Registration system has not been taken up in

the State due to lack of fund. However, the following steps were taken to create awareness

on registration of birth and death during 2022: -

  · District Collectors were requested to instruct Tahsildars / Municipal
       Commissioners to ensure display of posters in all public places and to instruct the
       Panchayat Secretaries.

  · Commissioner, Women & Child Department, was requested to issue instructions
       to Project Directors to arrange for distribution and display of posters at
       Anganwadi Centers and to ensure Anganwadi workers notify Births / Deaths
       regularly to village Secretaries.

  Arunachal Pradesh: Publicity measures were taken to create awareness among

the general public about the need, importance and benefits of births and deaths

registration via distribution of pamphlets through the students of various schools and

public on 26th January & 15th August 2022 and during the "Sarkar Apke Dwar Camps''

through displaying Banners/Posters in the stall of Economics & Statistics Department at

General Ground, various district during the period 2022.

  Bihar: The State Government carried out publicity on the importance of

registration of births and deaths by bringing out advertisements in daily newspapers from

time to time.

  Chhattisgarh: Publicity has been made through Pamphlets / Poster for birth and

death registration.

                     23
         Goa: Information regarding various services provided by the Births and Deaths

Registration Units, fees charged for these services, Timeline for delivery of services, etc.

have been displayed in all the offices of the civil registration functionaries for creating

awareness among the citizens.

         Gujarat: Publicity efforts were made by the State and District authorities through

various meetings and trainings to improve registration of births and deaths.

         Himachal Pradesh: Publicity has been made through distribution of Posters

among all the Sr. MS, BMOs, BDOs, & urban units in districts to make aware the general

public.

         Jharkhand: During the year publicity was carried out using Pamphlets, Banner of

Registration Birth & Death in all 9 Blocks, Hospital and Public Place etc. of District Godda.

         Karnataka: During the year 2022, wide publicity regarding importance of

registration of births & deaths were made through Newspapers and all publications

published by Directorate of Economics and Statistics. A full-fledged help desk with toll free

number 1800-425-6578 has been established for providing information on birth and

death registration in e-JanMa software and to resolve the grievances for the public.

         Kerala: In creating awareness among the public regarding the importance and

necessity of registration of vital events, various publicity measures were taken in the year

2022 such as: -

  · Arranged publicity measures through Doordarshan and All India radio.

  · Permanent Notice Boards were exhibited in front of all registration units.

  · Cinema Slides on the importance of registration of births and deaths were

         exhibited throughout the State in theatres.

  · Banners, posters and display boards on the importance of registration of births

         and deaths were provided in public places.

  · The Panchayat Department publication namely "Panchayat Raj" magazine plays a

         role in the publicity of civil registration activities.

  · The details of services rendered, name of designated officer, time limit for

         registering births/deaths and the duties of the public under Civil Registration

         System are included in the `Citizen's Charter' published by the Gram Panchayats

         and Municipalities under the Kerala Panchayat Raj Act 1994 and Kerala

         Municipality Act 1994.

         Maharashtra: Official correspondence to all Registrars for proper publicity in

their jurisdiction to create awareness in general public. An article narrating importance of

registration and procedure of registration of births & deaths has also been published in

`Arogya Patrika'.

  Mizoram: Adequate publicity has been made on the importance of Civil

Registration. Advertisements were often displayed in the daily newspapers and

periodicals in local language. Young Mizo Association (YMA), an NGO, and local

council/village council took up the responsibility of informing the public about the

importance reporting vital events to the Registrar of births and deaths.

of births and deaths, which were exhibited to the public in the form of posters, pamphlets,

wall hangers, bill boards etc.

  Odisha: Adequate publicity through Electronic media, All India Radio, Display of

Posters, Distribution of leaflets, screening of the films etc., has been done in the State

during the year on registration of birth and death to popularize the civil registration within

community.

  · The District Registrar and Local Registrars have been instructed to display

  important places in their offices for public awareness.

  · Instructions issued to display publicity material in the various Exhibitions

  organized by the department at block /village level.

  · Regular announcements on the importance of registration of births and deaths

  have been made through public address system of Village Gurudwaras.

  Paramedical staff also spread awareness in immunization camps during their

  routine visits.

  · Instructions were issued to District and Local Registrars to use CRS logo and

  message of birth and death registration in regular correspondence.

  Rajasthan: Publicity on registration of birth and death were made through Radio

Jingles at State level and through appeal made by District administration and distribution

  · A name board containing the name of the Registrar and the working time of the

  the availability of the Registrar inside the hospital premises.

  · To give wide publicity on the extension of time provided for name inclusion, a

  press release on the importance of name registration and the time limit

  extension was published in major News publications to enable the public to make

  use of this opportunity to insert the name in the birth record.

  · A sample IEC material was shared with the District authorities to make

  arrangements on the display of the IEC on name registration in the delivery

  wards and also the places where the public gatherings are more.

2.7. Issuance of birth and death extracts / certificates: -The Registrar under the
provision of Section 12 of the RBD Act, 1969 are required to give an extract of prescribed
particulars from the register relating to birth or death, free of charge to the person who
gives the information under Section 8 and 9 of the said Act. In addition to that, under
Section 17 of the RBD Act, the extract relating to birth or death (any number of copies) has
also been issued on the payment of prescribed fee to the person asking for it. The
information in respect of issuance of extract/certificate is given in Statement 6.

2.8. Difficulties encounter in implementation of the Act: Some of the States have
furnished the details of problems facing in implementing the provision of RBD Act and
State Rules in the State/UT. The brief points given by the State Governments in their annual
report is summarized below: -

  Andhra Pradesh: No fresh sanction of posts of Statistical staff was made in 2022.

The lack of budget under CRS and MCCD towards trainings, Publicity, inspection and printing of

forms affects the successful implementation of RBD act in the State.

  Arunachal Pradesh: Inadequate infrastructure is the major problem for

implementation of the Act. Further, due to shortage of vital statistics staff, lack of proper

communication, illiteracy among the villagers, villages being scattered in far flung areas,

etc. caused the issue of under reporting.

  Assam: Non-availability of CRS fund in State budget for implementation of RBD

Act, 1969. There is non-availability of net connectivity for supplied PCs and no initiatives

taken by different departments involved in implementation of RBD Act.

  Goa: All the Births & Deaths records from 1971 onwards have been computerized,

there is a possibility of damage to the physical Birth/Death records as the Registers

containing these records are worn out over the years.

  Gujarat: Some post such as Research Officer, Research Assistant and Statistical

Assistant are lying Vacant this may delay administrative/technical work.

  Himachal Pradesh: Local Registrars cum Panchayats Secretaries in concerned

Panchayats are not posting vital events online regularly. There is shortage of manpower in

some of the registration units across the State. Moreover, shortage of manpower in most

                                           28
of the health institutions across State and District Headquarter is also affecting the routine
working of online CRS.

         Jharkhand: Some difficulties are encountered in implementing the provisions of

RBD Act and Rules in respect of delayed registration provisions. Difficulties in timely

submission of reports and supervision due to lack of Block Statistical Supervisor at block

level. Difficulties under uniform CRS software in obtaining affidavit by general public for

reporting events after 30 days of occurrence.

         Karnataka: Inadequacy of staff at all levels. Lack of administrative authority of

the Chief Registrar over registration functionaries.

         Kerala: Pre-occupation of Registrars and other functionaries with other duties.

Damage caused to very old registration record. Non-registration of large chunk of infant

and child deaths particularly in rural areas. Inclusion of details without care in registration

by the public and request for correction of particulars in the registers as entered in other

documents pertaining to the individuals.

         Madhya Pradesh: Vacant posts of Assistant Statistical Officer, Statistical Clerks

and Clerks in various Municipal Corporations and Municipalities. Lack of control over

Notifiers in the rural areas.

         Maharashtra: In rural area due to illiteracy, lack of transportation in hilly areas,

and public unawareness are the hurdles in achieving the goal of 100% registration. The

Gram Sevaks who is Ex-officio Registrar of births and deaths, has to cover many villages

due to vacant posts.

         Mizoram: The geographic location of the State has affected the registration

process. Gap in communication and transportation facilities hampers timely reports

making it difficult to consistently maintain 100% level of reporting.

         Nagaland: Inadequate postal, transportation and communication facilities. Lack

of remuneration for CRS functionaries, delay in submission of reports by Registrar and low

level of awareness are major difficulties in the State.

         Odisha: There is shortage of staff/manpower in the State.

         Punjab: There is a shortage of Staff at every level in the State and associated

departments are facing difficulty in managing various affairs of registration of births and

deaths.

         Tamil Nadu: Non-availability of funds for sustaining the registration of births and

deaths through online facility in terms of software and hardware, inspection of registration

units, organizing training programme for CRS functionaries, Publicity on CRS at

State/Districts/Registration Unit levels etc.

                                               29
  Telangana: Lack of Sub registrars and statistical staff in Municipalities. Large

number of vacant posts of Panchayats Secretaries.

  Tripura: Shortage of Vital Statistical Staff at State level, posting of Staff at district

level lack of travelling allowance budget for contractual staff posted at State/District head

quarter inspection work of registration centers are affected.

  Uttarakhand: Lack of manpower, training, publicity, remote rural area and a lot

of posts are lying vacant in birth-death registration cadre of Health department are posing

difficulties in effective implementation of RBD Act, 1969.

  West Bengal: As such, there is no difficulty in the implementation of the act at the

field level. However, at times problem was faced in some places due to a shortage of

manpower.

  Puducherry: No difficulties encountered in implementing RBD Act 1969 since

compulsory Registration of vital events was prevalent during the French Rule.

           

           30
                                                   CHAPTER ­ III

                              A BRIEF ANALYSIS OF DATA ON REGISTERED
                                  BIRTHS, DEATHS AND INFANT DEATHS

3.1 Births and deaths are the two most important vital events in the life of an individual
and for the society as a whole. Based on the data collected under the Civil Registration
System on registered births and deaths, this chapter presents the trend in number of
registered events and other related indicators, at the State and National level.

3.2 In 2022, the number of registration units along with the level of reporting at the
national level and the States/UTs with rural/urban details may be seen at Statement 7. It
may be noted here that level of reporting indicates the status of returns of registered births
and deaths by the concerned Registrar within a State / UT during the year. It provides the
deficiency at a glance and helps in reviewing the registration system and taking measures
that would be necessary to improve the reporting levels, wherever required.

3.3 At the national level, 97.6% registration units reported monthly returns of registered
births and deaths to the District Registrar's or Chief Registrar's Office during 2022. For rural
areas, the reporting level are at 97.5% and for urban areas, it is 99.0%. However, these
figures do not include Arunachal Pradesh and Delhi as these States/UTs have provided only
partial information.

3.4 Cent percent level of reporting has been achieved by 24 States/UTs viz. Andhra
Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Goa, Gujarat, Haryana, Himachal Pradesh, Jharkhand, Karnataka,
