   3.6 The number of registered births has increased from 242.0 lakhs in 2021 to 254.4
lakhs in 2022 ­ an increase of about 5.1%. There has been an increase in the year 2022 in
registered births in almost all States/UTs except Bihar, Haryana, Himachal Pradesh, Sikkim,
West Bengal, Ladakh, and Lakshadweep as compared to 2021. Nine major States namely Uttar
Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra, Jharkhand, Karnataka, Gujarat, Telangana,
Chhattisgarh and Assam have contributed significantly to the increase in registered births in
2022 over 2021. On the other hand, decrease in registered births in six figures has been
observed in two major States namely, West Bengal and Bihar during 2021-2022.

                                                               37
3.9 Registration of Birth & Death within the prescribed time limit of 21 days

 3.9.1 The registered births and deaths have been classified in four categories depending upon
 their period of registration. The four time periods considered for the purpose are: i) within the
 prescribed time limit (up to 21 days), ii) After 21 days but within 30 days, iii) After 30 days but
 within 1 year, and iv) More than 1 year. For 2022, Thirty-five (35) States/UTs have provided
 data on Births and Deaths Registration within the prescribed time limit (i.e. within 21 days).
 Partial information has been submitted by Sikkim and therefore the same has not been taken
 into account while consolidating the data for Time gap in the Registration of Births & Deaths
 tables.

3.9.2 On the basis of data received from States/UTs the percentage of registration achieved out
of the total registration undertaken in 2022 within the prescribed time limit (21 days) is as
under:

3.9.3 It has been observed that 13 States/UTs have achieved more than 90% registration of
births within the prescribed time limit of 21 days. Further, 4 States are in the category of more
than 80 to less than or equal to 90 percent, 14 States/UTs are in the category of more than 50 to
less than or equal to 80 percent, and the remaining 4 States/UTs are under the category of less
than or equal to 50 percent.

3.9.4 Statement 12 also shows the percentage of deaths registered within the prescribed time
limit of 21 days to the total deaths registered in the state and arranged in descending order. 9
States/UTs have achieved more than 90% registration of death within the prescribed time limit
of 21 days. Other 9 States/UTs fall in the category of more than 80 to less than or equal to 90
percent, 12 States/UTs are in the category of more than 50 to less than or equal to 80 percent
and the remaining 5 States/UTs are under the category of less than or equal to 50 percent in
death registration within the prescribed time limit of 21 days. Chart 3 & 4 shows separately the
percentage of Births and Deaths registered within the prescribed time period of 21 days .

3.9.5 Chart 5 shows the performance of major States in terms of percentage registration done
within prescribed time i.e 21 days. In 8 States namely, Tamil Nadu, Haryana, Punjab, Madhya
Pradesh, Maharashtra, Gujarat, Chhattisgarh and Andhra Pradesh, the share of registered births
and deaths within 21 days was more than 80 percent of the total registered births and Deaths.
The share of Telangana and West Bengal was between 70 to 80 percent. In case of Odisha, the
share of timely registration was between 90-100 percent in case of births and was between 70
to 80 percent in case of deaths. In case of Bihar and Assam, the share of within Time Birth
Registration was between 70 to 80 percent while in case of death, it was slightly lower and was
between 60 and 70 percent. In case of Uttar Pradesh and Karnataka, the share of both Timely
birth and death registration was in the range of 60-70 percent.

3.12 Rural-Urban wise composition of Registered Births and Deaths, 2022

3.12.1 Statement 17 gives the Rural-Urban wise distribution of registered births and deaths.
Rural-Urban wise distribution of registered birth reveals that the share of births registered in
Urban areas is about 13 percent more than that of births registered in rural areas. While in case
of Registered deaths, the share of rural areas is about 19 percent more than that of Urban areas.

3.13. Rural-Urban wise Composition of Registered Births and Deaths of Major States
3.13.1 The Rural- Urban composition of Registered Births and Deaths by major States are
provided in Statement 18. The data on Rural-Urban distribution of birth and death registrations
reveals that the share of Rural registration is more in Assam, Bihar, Jharkhand and Uttar Pradesh
than the share of Births registered in Urban areas. In remaining 15 Major States, the share of
Births registration is higher in case of Urban areas. On the other hand, in the case of Death
Registration, the share of Rural Registration is higher in almost all major States except
Maharashtra and Telangana.

3.16 Still Births

3.16.1 The World Health Organization (WHO) defines a still birth as a baby born with no signs
of life at or after 28 weeks' gestation. Still birth, also known as foetal death, has been defined as
`death prior to the complete expulsion or extraction from its mother of a product of human
conception, irrespective of the duration of pregnancy and which is not an induced termination of
pregnancy. The death is indicated by the fact that after such expulsion or extraction, the fetus does
not breathe or show any other evidence of life, such as beating of the heart, pulsation of the
umbilical cord, or definite movement of voluntary muscles. Heartbeats are to be distinguished from
transient cardiac contractions; stillbirths are to be distinguished from fleeting respiratory efforts
or gasps.'

3.16.2 Statement 21 presents still births registered by place of occurrence (Rural /Urban) of the
event for the year 2022. It may be observed that in total registered still births, share of rural
registered still births is only 41.5%, while this share is about 58.5% in Urban Areas. State/UT-
wise registered still births have been depicted in Table 4 (Page No. 64).

             Statement 21: Still Births registered during 2022
